FILE docs/architecture.md
0001: # System Architecture & Compiler Design
0002: 
0003: This document details the software architecture, compilation pipeline, symbol table structure, intermediate representation (IR), and execution model of the Data Science Pipeline DSL compiler (`ds_pipeline_dsl`).
0004: 
0005: ---
0006: 
0007: ## 1. Compiler Flow Architecture
0008: 
0009: The compiler implements a classic multi-stage pipeline with strict phase decoupling. Each stage is a pure function of its inputs, transforming the source representation into progressively lower representations:
0010: 
0011: ### 1.1 Mermaid Pipeline Flowchart
0012: *(Note: You can copy this diagram or export it to an image tool.)*
0013: 
0014: ```mermaid
0015: flowchart TD
0016:     Source[".pipe Source File"] --> Lexer["1. Lexer (tokenize)"]
0017:     Lexer -->|LexicalError (exit 1)| LexErr["Error Output"]
0018:     Lexer -->|Tokens| Parser["2. Parser (recursive descent)"]
0019:     Parser -->|SyntaxError_ (exit 1)| SynErr["Error Output"]
0020:     Parser -->|AST (PipelineNode)| Semantic["3. Semantic Analyzer (analyze)"]
0021:     Semantic -->|SemanticError (exit 1)| SemErr["Error Output"]
0022:     Semantic -->|Symbol Table + Valid AST| Lowering["4. IR Lowering (lower_to_ir)"]
0023:     Lowering -->|Raw IR List| Optimizer["5. Optimizer (optimize)"]
0024:     
0025:     subgraph OptimizerPasses ["Optimizer Passes (O(n) each)"]
0026:         Pass1["Pass 1: _remove_unreachable"] --> Pass2["Pass 2: _remove_dead_trains"]
0027:         Pass2 --> Pass3["Pass 3: _merge_adjacent_drops"]
0028:         Pass3 --> Pass4["Pass 4: _collapse_duplicates"]
0029:     end
0030:     
0031:     Optimizer --> OptimizerPasses
0032:     OptimizerPasses -->|Optimized IR List + Notes| Executor["6. Symbolic Executor (execute)"]
0033:     Executor --> Trace["Human-Readable Execution Trace"]
0034: 
0035:     CLI["CLI Driver (cli.py)"] -.->|Coordinates & Formats| Source
0036:     CLI -.->|Optionally displays| Lexer
0037:     CLI -.->|Optionally displays| Parser
0038:     CLI -.->|Optionally displays| Lowering
0039:     CLI -.->|Optionally displays| Optimizer
0040:     CLI -.->|Optionally displays| Executor
0041: ```
0042: 
0043: ### 1.2 ASCII Architecture Fallback
0044: ```text
0045:   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0046:   |                             .pipe Source File                           |
0047:   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0048:                                        |
0049:                                        v
0050:   +--------------------+    Raises LexicalError
0051:   |    1. Lexer        | ------------------------> [Exit 1: Clean Message]
0052:   |  (lexer.tokenize)  |
0053:   +--------------------+
0054:            | Tokens
0055:            v
0056:   +--------------------+    Raises SyntaxError_
0057:   |    2. Parser       | ------------------------> [Exit 1: Clean Message]
0058:   |  (parser.Parser)   |
0059:   +--------------------+
0060:            | Abstract Syntax Tree (AST)
0061:            v
0062:   +--------------------+    Raises SemanticError
0063:   |    3. Semantic     | ------------------------> [Exit 1: Clean Message]
0064:   | (semantic.analyze) | <--> SymbolTable (Tracks state & dependency rules)
0065:   +--------------------+
0066:            | Validated AST + SymbolTable
0067:            v
0068:   +--------------------+
0069:   |  4. IR Lowering    | Converts AST statements to flat IRInstruction list
0070:   |  (ir.lower_to_ir)  |
0071:   +--------------------+
0072:            | Raw IR Instructions
0073:            v
0074:   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0075:   |  5. Optimizer (optimizer.optimize)                                      |
0076:   |     - Pass 1: _remove_unreachable (tail after last OP_EVAL)             |
0077:   |     - Pass 2: _remove_dead_trains (models never evaluated)             |
0078:   |     - Pass 3: _merge_adjacent_drops (contiguous drop runs merged)       |
0079:   |     - Pass 4: _collapse_duplicates (consecutive identical clean/select)|
0080:   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0081:            | Optimized IR Instructions (+ Optimization Notes)
0082:            v
0083:   +--------------------+
0084:   | 6. Symbolic Engine | Walks IR and generates simulated accuracy/execution
0085:   | (executor.execute) | trace messages without performing heavy computation
0086:   +--------------------+
0087:            |
0088:            v
0089:   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0090:   |                        Output Trace / CLI Display                       |
0091:   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0092: ```
0093: 
0094: ---
0095: 
0096: ## 2. Module Responsibilities & Key Symbols
0097: 
0098: | Module | Responsibility | Key Classes / Functions |
0099: |:---|:---|:---|
0100: | [`lexer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/lexer.py) | Scans raw string source into token stream using master regex matching; tracks line and column coordinates; skips whitespace and `//` comments. | `Token`, `LexicalError`, `tokenize(source)` |
0101: | [`parser.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/parser.py) | LL(1) recursive-descent parser; enforces grammar rules, single-pipeline scoping, and delimiters; produces typed AST nodes. | `SyntaxError_`, `Parser`, `Parser.parse_pipeline()` |
0102: | [`ast_nodes.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ast_nodes.py) | Typed dataclasses representing language statements and top-level pipeline declarations. | `PipelineNode`, `LoadStmt`, `CleanStmt`, `SelectStmt`, `DropStmt`, `SplitStmt`, `TrainStmt`, `EvaluateStmt` |
0103: | [`semantic.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py) | Verifies pipeline state dependencies, single-load, split boundaries, column sets, disjointness, and evaluation life-cycles. | `SymbolTable`, `SemanticError`, `analyze(pipeline_ast)` |
0104: | [`ir.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ir.py) | Defines the intermediate representation dataclass and lowers validated AST statements into a flat sequence of IR instructions. | `IRInstruction(op, args)`, `lower_to_ir(pipeline_ast, symbol_table)` |
0105: | [`optimizer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/optimizer.py) | Performs 4 independent, linear-time $O(n)$ optimization passes; records diagnostic optimization notes. | `optimize(ir_list, notes)`, `_remove_unreachable()`, `_remove_dead_trains()`, `_merge_adjacent_drops()`, `_collapse_duplicates()` |
0106: | [`executor.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/executor.py) | Symbolic execution engine; walks IR instructions and generates formatted trace logs simulating pipeline execution. | `execute(ir_list)` |
0107: | [`cli.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/cli.py) | CLI entry-point using `argparse`; handles command-line arguments, orchestrates compiler passes, and prints formatted sections. | `main()` |
0108: 
0109: ---
0110: 
0111: ## 3. Symbol Table State Tracking
0112: 
0113: The `SymbolTable` maintains contextual pipeline state during semantic analysis. Because pipelines represent directed data workflows, later operations depend on prior stage artifacts:
0114: 
0115: | Field Name | Type | Description / Tracked Invariant |
0116: |:---|:---|:---|
0117: | `data_loaded` | `bool` | Set to `True` when the first `load` statement executes. Required before any `clean`, `select`, `drop`, or `split`. |
0118: | `load_line` | `Optional[int]` | Records the line number of the dataset `load` to provide precise diagnostic messages if duplicate loads appear. |
0119: | `data_cleaned` | `bool` | Set to `True` when at least one `clean` statement executes. |
0120: | `selected_columns` | `Optional[list[str]]` | Tracks the currently active selected column list. Updated by `select` and reduced when `drop` removes selected columns. |
0121: | `dropped_columns` | `dict[str, int]` | Mapping of `column_name -> line_number` for every dropped column. Enforces that dropped columns cannot be re-dropped or selected. |
0122: | `splits` | `dict[str, float]` | Mapping of `split_name -> percentage`. Enforces unique split names, `0 < percent < 100`, and cumulative $\le 100\%$. |
0123: | `split_lines` | `dict[str, int]` | Records definition line number for each split partition. |
0124: | `models` | `dict[str, str]` | Mapping of `model_name -> algorithm`. Enforces unique model names and verifies models exist before `evaluate`. |
0125: | `model_lines` | `dict[str, int]` | Records definition line number for each trained model. |
0126: | `first_train` | `Optional[tuple[str, int]]` | Stores `(model_name, line)` of the very first `train` statement. Used to reject data-preparation statements (`clean`, `select`, `drop`) occurring after model training. |
0127: 
0128: ---
0129: 
0130: ## 4. Intermediate Representation (IR) Specification
0131: 
0132: The Intermediate Representation is a flat, linear instruction list consisting of `IRInstruction` dataclasses:
0133: ```python
0134: @dataclass
0135: class IRInstruction:
0136:     op: str       # Opcode identifier
0137:     args: dict    # Instruction arguments, including source line coordinate
0138: ```
0139: 
0140: ### IR Opcode Table
0141: 
0142: | Opcode | Arguments (`args` dict) | Description / Example Lowering |
0143: |:---|:---|:---|
0144: | `OP_LOAD` | `{"filename": str, "line": int}` | Loads dataset source. Example: `args={'filename': 'data.csv', 'line': 2}` |
0145: | `OP_CLEAN` | `{"strategy": str, "line": int}` | Applies cleaning strategy. Example: `args={'strategy': 'missing_values', 'line': 3}` |
0146: | `OP_SELECT` | `{"columns": list[str], "line": int}` | Specifies active feature columns. Example: `args={'columns': ['cgpa', 'attendance'], 'line': 4}` |
0147: | `OP_DROP` | `{"columns": list[str], "line": int}` | Removes columns. Example: `args={'columns': ['student_id'], 'line': 5}` |
0148: | `OP_SPLIT` | `{"name": str, "percent": float, "line": int}` | Configures dataset partition. Example: `args={'name': 'train', 'percent': 80.0, 'line': 6}` |
0149: | `OP_TRAIN` | `{"model_name": str, "algorithm": str, "line": int}` | Instantiates model. Example: `args={'model_name': 'm1', 'algorithm': 'decision_tree', 'line': 7}` |
0150: | `OP_EVAL` | `{"model_name": str, "line": int}` | Triggers evaluation. Example: `args={'model_name': 'm1', 'line': 8}` |
0151: 
0152: ---
0153: 
0154: ## 5. Optimizer Architecture & Safety Model
0155: 
0156: The optimizer transforms the linear IR through four independent passes in strict $O(n)$ time:
0157: 1. `_remove_unreachable`: Removes any dead tail instructions appearing after the final `OP_EVAL`.
0158: 2. `_remove_dead_trains`: Backward scan identifying models that are never evaluated; eliminates unused `OP_TRAIN` instructions.
0159: 3. `_merge_adjacent_drops`: Combines immediately adjacent runs of `OP_DROP` instructions into a single drop with deduplicated columns.
0160: 4. `_collapse_duplicates`: Removes consecutive identical `OP_CLEAN` or `OP_SELECT` instructions (excluding line numbers from comparison).
0161: 
0162: ### Safety Argument
0163: - **Observable Behavior**: In this DSL, the observable behavior of a program is defined strictly as the sequence of model evaluations produced at `OP_EVAL` points:
0164:   $$\text{Record} = (\text{model\_name}, \text{algorithm}, \text{train\_state}, \text{eval\_state})$$
0165: - Instructions appearing after the *last* evaluation cannot affect any evaluation record, making their removal safe.
0166: - `OP_TRAIN` writes only to internal model state read solely by `OP_EVAL` of that model name. If no evaluation ever reads that model, eliminating the train leaves all evaluation records unchanged.
0167: - Data-preparation idempotence (repeated clean or select) and set-union commutativity (adjacent drops) preserve exact state transitions.
0168: - All four passes operate without mutating input IR structures, preserving data integrity.

0140: 
0141: ## Part 2: Viva Quick Reference
0142: 
0143: ### 1. Where Compiler Concepts Live in Code
0144: | Compiler Phase / Concept | Source Location | Implementation Details |
0145: |:---|:---|:---|
0146: | **Lexical Analysis (Scanner)** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/lexer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/lexer.py) | `tokenize()` uses a single compiled master regex `MASTER_PATTERN` matching 13 token patterns; tracks line/column numbers. |
0147: | **Parsing (Recursive Descent)** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/parser.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/parser.py) | LL(1) `Parser` class with `peek()`, `advance()`, `expect()`, and statement parsers for each grammar production. |
0148: | **Abstract Syntax Tree (AST)** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ast_nodes.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ast_nodes.py) | Python `@dataclass` nodes (`PipelineNode`, `LoadStmt`, `DropStmt`, etc.) preserving statement line numbers. |
0149: | **Semantic Analysis & State** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py) | `SymbolTable` and `analyze()` enforce 21 semantic rules across loaded data, partition splits, column sets, and models. |
0150: | **Intermediate Representation** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ir.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ir.py) | Flat linear instruction list: `IRInstruction(op, args)` generated by `lower_to_ir()`. |
0151: | **Code Optimization** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/optimizer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/optimizer.py) | Four independent linear-time passes: `_remove_unreachable`, `_remove_dead_trains`, `_merge_adjacent_drops`, `_collapse_duplicates`. |
0152: | **Symbolic Execution** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/executor.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/executor.py) | `execute()` simulates pipeline operations and produces human-readable trace logs without invoking external ML libraries. |
0153: | **Driver & CLI** | [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/cli.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/cli.py) | `main()` coordinates the pipeline stages, parses command-line flags, and handles clean error reporting. |
0154: 
0155: ---
0156: 
0157: ### 2. Semantic Rules Map
0158: In [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py), every rule is enforced during the single AST walk in `analyze()`:
0159: - **`LoadStmt`** (lines 28–40): Rejects duplicate load (`table.data_loaded`) and empty filename (`stmt.filename.strip() == ""`).
0160: - **`CleanStmt`** (lines 41–54): Rejects clean before load (`not table.data_loaded`) and clean after train (`table.first_train`).
0161: - **`SelectStmt`** (lines 55–83): Rejects select before load, select after train, duplicate columns in select list, and selection of previously dropped columns (`col in table.dropped_columns`).
0162: - **`DropStmt`** (lines 84–130): Rejects drop before load, drop after train, duplicate column in drop list, dropping an already dropped column, dropping a column not in current selection, and dropping all selected columns.
0163: - **`SplitStmt`** (lines 131–153): Rejects split before load, split percent $\le 0$ or $\ge 100$, duplicate split name, and cumulative split total $> 100\%$.
0164: - **`TrainStmt`** (lines 154–170): Rejects training when `train` split is undefined, and duplicate model names.
0165: - **`EvaluateStmt`** (lines 171–177): Rejects evaluating a model that has not been trained (`stmt.model_name not in table.models`).
0166: 
0167: ---
0168: 
0169: ### 3. How to Add a New Statement to the Compiler (Worked Example: `drop`)
0170: Adding a statement requires walking sequentially through each compiler layer:
0171: 1. **Lexer** ([`lexer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/lexer.py)):
0172:    - Add `"drop"` to the `KEYWORDS` set.
0173:    - Scanner automatically emits `Token("DROP", "drop", line, col)`.
0174: 2. **AST** ([`ast_nodes.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ast_nodes.py)):
0175:    - Define `@dataclass class DropStmt: columns: list[str]; line: int`.
0176: 3. **Parser** ([`parser.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/parser.py)):
0177:    - In `parse_statement()`, add `if tok.type == "DROP": ...` to parse comma-separated column identifiers and semicolon.
0178: 4. **Semantic Analyzer** ([`semantic.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py)):
0179:    - Add `isinstance(stmt, DropStmt)` branch.
0180:    - Check preconditions (`table.data_loaded`, `not table.first_train`, column validity).
0181:    - Update `table.dropped_columns` and update/filter `table.selected_columns`.
0182: 5. **IR Lowering** ([`ir.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/ir.py)):
0183:    - Lower `DropStmt` into `IRInstruction("OP_DROP", {"columns": stmt.columns, "line": stmt.line})`.
0184: 6. **Optimizer** ([`optimizer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/optimizer.py)):
0185:    - If adjacent drops appear, merge their column lists via `_merge_adjacent_drops()`.
0186: 7. **Executor** ([`executor.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/executor.py)):
0187:    - In `execute()`, handle `instr.op == "OP_DROP"` and emit `Dropped columns: {cols}`.
0188: 
0189: ---
0190: 
0191: ### 4. How to Add a New Semantic Rule
0192: To add a new semantic constraint (e.g. "disallow evaluating the same model more than three times"):
0193: 1. Open [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/semantic.py).
0194: 2. Add a tracking field to `SymbolTable.__init__` (e.g., `self.eval_counts: dict[str, int] = {}`).
0195: 3. In `analyze()`, under the corresponding statement branch (e.g. `elif isinstance(stmt, EvaluateStmt):`), inspect the state:
0196:    ```python
0197:    count = table.eval_counts.get(stmt.model_name, 0)
0198:    if count >= 3:
0199:        raise SemanticError(f"Semantic Error at line {stmt.line}: model '{stmt.model_name}' evaluated more than 3 times.")
0200:    table.eval_counts[stmt.model_name] = count + 1
0201:    ```
0202: 4. Add corresponding pass/fail test cases to [`tests/test_semantic.py`](../tests/test_semantic.py).
0203: 
0204: ---
0205: 
0206: ### 5. The Optimizer Safety Argument in Five Lines
0207: 1. **Observable Equivalence**: The observable result of a pipeline is defined strictly by the evaluation records produced at `OP_EVAL` statements.
0208: 2. **Unreachable Code**: Any instructions appearing after the *last* `OP_EVAL` cannot alter any evaluation record and are safely deleted.
0209: 3. **Dead Training Elimination**: Unused `OP_TRAIN` instructions write only to model entries never read by any `OP_EVAL`; removing them produces zero observable trace difference.
0210: 4. **Data-Preparation Independence**: Semantic rules strictly prohibit data-prep statements (`clean`, `select`, `drop`) after training; removing dead trains can never bring data-prep statements adjacent.
0211: 5. **Local Idempotence**: Consecutive duplicate cleans, duplicate selects, and adjacent drops are idempotent under set and filtering semantics, preserving exact pipeline state transitions.

FILE docs/grammar.md
0001: # Language Grammar & Lexical Specification
0002: 
0003: This document provides the formal grammar specification and lexical analysis definition for the Data Science Pipeline Domain-Specific Language (`ds_pipeline_dsl`). The grammar matches [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/parser.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/parser.py) and [`src/ds_pipeline_dsl/lexer.py`](../src/ds_pipeline_dsl/lexer.py) exactly.
0004: 
0005: ---
0006: 
0007: ## 1. Lexical Specification
0008: 
0009: ### 1.1 Keywords
0010: The following 8 words are reserved keywords. When encountered by the scanner as whole words, they emit their corresponding token types rather than generic `IDENT` tokens:
0011: 
0012: | Keyword | Token Type | Purpose |
0013: |:---|:---|:---|
0014: | `pipeline` | `PIPELINE` | Declares the top-level pipeline block |
0015: | `load` | `LOAD` | Loads a dataset source file |
0016: | `clean` | `CLEAN` | Applies a data cleaning strategy |
0017: | `select` | `SELECT` | Specifies feature columns |
0018: | `drop` | `DROP` | Removes columns from the pipeline |
0019: | `split` | `SPLIT` | Defines data partition percentages |
0020: | `train` | `TRAIN` | Trains a model with a specified algorithm |
0021: | `evaluate` | `EVALUATE` | Evaluates a trained model |
0022: 
0023: > **Contextual Keyword Exception**: In a `split` statement, the split name accepts either an `IDENT` or the `TRAIN` keyword (`split_name ::= IDENT | 'train'`). This allows statements such as `split train = 80%;` without ambiguity. In all other contexts, keywords are reserved and cannot be used as column, dataset, or model identifiers.
0024: 
0025: ### 1.2 Token Specification
0026: Tokens are matched using Python regular expressions compiled into a single master pattern scanning from left to right:
0027: 
0028: | Token Name | Regular Expression | Description / Semantic Value |
0029: |:---|:---|:---|
0030: | `STRING` | `"[^"\n]*"` | Double-quoted ASCII/UTF-8 string without linebreaks. The parser strips enclosing quotes. |
0031: | `BAD_STRING` | `"[^"\n]*` | Unterminated string literal reaching a newline or EOF. Raises `LexicalError`. |
0032: | `PERCENT` | `\d+(\.\d+)?%` | Numeric percentage value followed immediately by `%`. Stripped and parsed as a `float`. |
0033: | `NUMBER` | `\d+(\.\d+)?` | Raw numeric literal. Used to provide precise syntax diagnostics when `%` is omitted. |
0034: | `LBRACE` | `\{` | Left opening brace `{` |
0035: | `RBRACE` | `\}` | Right closing brace `}` |
0036: | `EQUALS` | `=` | Assignment operator `=` |
0037: | `SEMI` | `;` | Statement terminator `;` |
0038: | `COMMA` | `,` | Delimiter `,` separating column identifiers |
0039: | `IDENT` | `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*` | Identifier starting with a letter or underscore, followed by alphanumeric characters or underscores |
0040: | `SKIP` | `[ \t\r\n]+` | Whitespace (spaces, tabs, carriage returns `\r`, and line feeds `\n`), silently skipped |
0041: | `COMMENT` | `//[^\n]*` | Single-line comment starting with `//` and extending to end of line, silently skipped |
0042: | `MISMATCH` | `.` | Any other character not matched by above rules. Raises `LexicalError`. |
0043: | `EOF` | `""` | Synthetic End-Of-File marker appended when scanner reaches the end of the input string |
0044: 
0045: ### 1.3 Lexical Error Rules
0046: The lexer is strict and fails immediately upon encountering the first invalid token:
0047: 1. **Unterminated String**: If an opening quote `"` is not closed on the same line, `LexicalError` is raised:
0048:    ```text
0049:    Lexical Error at line {line_num}, col {col}: unterminated string literal
0050:    ```
0051: 2. **Unexpected Character**: Any character outside the recognized alphabet (e.g. `@`, `#`, `$`, `-`, or non-ASCII characters outside comments and strings) raises `LexicalError`:
0052:    ```text
0053:    Lexical Error at line {line_num}, col {col}: unexpected character {value!r}
0054:    ```
0055: 
0056: ---
0057: 
0058: ## 2. Syntactic Grammar (EBNF)
0059: 
0060: The language syntax is formally specified in Extended Backus-Naur Form (EBNF):
0061: 
0062: ```ebnf
0063: (* Top-level Program: exactly one pipeline per file *)
0064: pipeline_decl    ::= 'pipeline' IDENT '{' statement* '}'
0065: 
0066: (* Statements *)
0067: statement        ::= load_stmt
0068:                    | clean_stmt
0069:                    | select_stmt
0070:                    | drop_stmt
0071:                    | split_stmt
0072:                    | train_stmt
0073:                    | evaluate_stmt
0074: 
0075: (* Statement Definitions *)
0076: load_stmt        ::= 'load' STRING ';'
0077: clean_stmt       ::= 'clean' IDENT ';'
0078: select_stmt      ::= 'select' IDENT (',' IDENT)* ';'
0079: drop_stmt        ::= 'drop' IDENT (',' IDENT)* ';'
0080: split_stmt       ::= 'split' split_name '=' PERCENT ';'
0081: split_name       ::= IDENT | 'train'
0082: train_stmt       ::= 'train' IDENT '=' IDENT ';'
0083: evaluate_stmt    ::= 'evaluate' IDENT ';'
0084: ```
0085: 
0086: ---
0087: 
0088: ## 3. Syntactic Constraints & Parsing Rules
0089: 
0090: The recursive-descent parser enforces the following structural constraints:
0091: 1. **Single Pipeline per File**: Only one `pipeline` declaration is permitted per source file. Any trailing tokens or secondary pipeline blocks after the closing `}` raise `SyntaxError_`.
0092: 2. **Mandatory Block Delimiters**: The pipeline body must be enclosed in `{` and `}`. Missing braces or an unexpected EOF within the body raise `SyntaxError_`.
0093: 3. **Mandatory Statement Semicolons**: Every statement inside the pipeline body must end with a semicolon `;`. Standalone empty semicolons are prohibited.
0094: 4. **Comma-Separated Identifiers**: In `select` and `drop` statements, identifiers must be separated by commas `,`. Trailing commas (e.g. `select c1, ;`) or missing column names raise `SyntaxError_`.
0095: 5. **Mandatory Percentage Sign**: The `split` percentage must include the `%` suffix. A bare number (e.g. `80`) raises a syntax error:
0096:    ```text
0097:    Syntax Error at line {line}: expected PERCENT, found NUMBER ('80')
0098:    ```
0099: 6. **Identifiers and Strategy/Algorithm Free Names**: Strategy names in `clean` and algorithm names in `train` are free identifiers conforming to `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`.

FILE docs/performance.md
0001: # Compiler Performance Benchmark Report
0002: 
0003: ## Environment
0004: - Python version: 3.14.7
0005: - Platform: Windows (Windows-11-10.0.26200-SP0)
0006: - Benchmark run timestamp: 2026-10-22 12:06:40
0007: - Total wall-clock duration: 0.63 s
0008: 
0009: ## Benchmark Template & Optimization Effects
0010: The benchmark evaluates scalable synthetic data science pipelines generated by `build_program(n)`.
0011: The template systematically exercises all compiler front-end stages and optimizer transformations:
0012: - Consecutive `clean` statements: adjacent duplicates collapsed into one.
0013: - Multiple `drop` statements: consecutive adjacent drop statements merged into a single drop with deduplicated columns.
0014: - Consecutive `select` statements: adjacent duplicates collapsed into one.
0015: - Multi-unit model definitions: each unit defines a model that is evaluated and an unused model that is never evaluated;
0016:   the dead-train elimination pass removes all unused models.
0017: - Trailing model train: placed after the final evaluate statement; removed by the unreachable-code elimination pass.
0018: 
0019: ## Safeguards & Methodology
0020: Benchmark safeguards prevent hangs or budget overruns: sizes run in ascending order with quadratic projection
0021: checks enforcing a total time budget of 120 s; single repetitions are capped at 30 s;
0022: source sizes are bounded at 2000000 characters and size parameter at 20000; each size performs an untimed
0023: warm-up pass and correctness validation before timing; garbage collection is disabled during timed regions and run between repetitions;
0024: inner timing loops scale inversely with statement count to minimize timer quantization noise.
0025: 
0026: ## Results Table
0027: | Statements | Tokens | Lexer ms | Parser ms | Semantic ms | IR ms | Optimizer ms | Exec(opt) ms | Exec(unopt) ms | Total ms | us/stmt | IR before | IR after | Reduction % | Status |
0028: |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|:---|
0029: | 13 | 66 | 0.060 | 0.012 | 0.004 | 0.005 | 0.015 | 0.001 | 0.002 | 0.098 | 7.54 | 13 | 8 | 38.5% | ok |
0030: | 99 | 428 | 0.421 | 0.080 | 0.029 | 0.046 | 0.085 | 0.010 | 0.016 | 0.671 | 6.78 | 99 | 60 | 39.4% | ok |
0031: | 999 | 4208 | 4.428 | 0.842 | 0.315 | 0.452 | 0.818 | 0.098 | 0.163 | 6.952 | 6.96 | 999 | 600 | 39.9% | ok |
0032: | 9999 | 42008 | 45.302 | 8.014 | 2.955 | 5.082 | 10.714 | 1.494 | 2.519 | 73.560 | 7.36 | 9999 | 6000 | 40.0% | ok |
0033: 
0034: ## Observations
0035: 1. Scaling between 999 and 9999 statements: statement count grew by 10.01x while total execution time grew by 10.58x.
0036: 2. Microseconds per statement ratio between 999 and 9999 statements is 1.06 (approximately linear).
0037: 3. Dominant stage at 9999 statements: Lexer (45.302 ms, 61.6% of total pipeline time).
0038: 4. Execution speedup from optimization at 9999 statements: 1.69x (unoptimized 2.519 ms vs optimized 1.494 ms).
0039: 5. Status: all configured benchmark sizes completed successfully with full repetitions.

FILE docs/phase2_report.md
0001: # Data Science Pipeline DSL - Phase 2 Report
0002: 
0003: ## 1. Project Overview
0004: The Data Science Pipeline DSL (Domain-Specific Language) is a custom compiler frontend designed to simplify and standardize the creation of machine learning pipelines. It addresses the complexity and boilerplate of setting up end-to-end data workflows by providing a clean, declarative language tailored specifically for tasks like loading datasets, cleaning values, selecting features, creating train/test splits, and evaluating machine learning models.
0005: 
0006: ## 2. Phase 1 Recap
0007: Before Phase 2, the project existed as a single-file prototype (`initial_prototype.py`) which grouped the lexer, parser, AST structures, and a semantic analyzer into one monolithic script. This early iteration proved the viability of the language grammar and logic but lacked modularity, intermediate representations, optimizations, an executor, and testing infrastructure.
0008: 
0009: ## 3. Phase 2 Scope & Objectives
0010: The objectives of Phase 2 focused on transforming the prototype into a structured, modular compiler architecture. The goals were to:
0011: - Refactor the monolith into a proper Python package (`ds_pipeline_dsl`) with separated modules.
0012: - Introduce an Intermediate Representation (IR) generation stage (`lower_to_ir`).
0013: - Implement an Optimizer to collapse redundant statements and drop unreachable code.
0014: - Create a Symbolic Executor to provide a human-readable execution trace.
0015: - Expose a fully-featured Command-Line Interface (`cli.py`) for inspecting each compiler stage.
0016: - Establish a rigorous automated test suite covering all compiler stages and error states.
0017: 
0018: ## 4. System Architecture
0019: The pipeline now follows a standard multi-pass compiler architecture:
0020: 
0021: ```mermaid
0022: flowchart TD
0023:     A[DSL Source Code .pipe] --> B(Lexer)
0024:     B -->|Tokens| C(Parser)
0025:     C -->|AST| D(Semantic Analyzer)
0026:     D -->|Validated AST & Symbol Table| E(IR Lowering)
0027:     E -->|IR Instructions| F(Optimizer)
0028:     F -->|Optimized IR| G(Symbolic Executor)
0029:     G -->|Execution Trace| H[CLI / Output]
0030: ```
0031: 
0032: ## 5. Module-wise Implementation Details
0033: 
0034: ### `__init__.py`
0035: - **Purpose**: Marks the `src/ds_pipeline_dsl/` directory as a Python package.
0036: 
0037: ### `ast_nodes.py`
0038: - **Purpose**: Defines the Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) node structures.
0039: - **Data Structures**: Dataclasses like `PipelineNode`, `LoadStmt`, `CleanStmt`, `SplitStmt`, `TrainStmt`, `EvaluateStmt`.
0040: - **Core Logic**: Provides purely structural representation of a parsed pipeline and its statements.
0041: - **Interaction**: Instantiated by the parser and traversed by the semantic analyzer and IR lowerer.
0042: 
0043: ### `lexer.py`
0044: - **Purpose**: Converts a raw string of DSL code into a sequential stream of tokens.
0045: - **Data Structures**: A `Token` namedtuple and a list of regex rules defining token patterns.
0046: - **Core Logic**: Applies regex matches iteratively over the input string, discarding whitespace/comments and emitting `Token` objects. Raises a `LexicalError` on invalid characters.
0047: - **Interaction**: Feeds the resulting token list into the parser.
0048: 
0049: ### `parser.py`
0050: - **Purpose**: Constructs an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) from the token stream.
0051: - **Data Structures**: A recursive-descent `Parser` class.
0052: - **Core Logic**: Uses `peek()`, `advance()`, and `expect()` methods to match tokens against the expected DSL grammar. Raises a `SyntaxError_` if the tokens violate the grammar.
0053: - **Interaction**: Takes input from the lexer and passes its output (`PipelineNode`) to the semantic analyzer.
0054: 
0055: ### `semantic.py`
0056: - **Purpose**: Validates the logical correctness of the AST.
0057: - **Data Structures**: `SymbolTable` to track defined datasets, splits, and models.
0058: - **Core Logic**: Traverses the AST statements, ensuring operations are logically ordered (e.g., you cannot `train` before `split` and percentages must strictly fall between 0 and 100). Raises a `SemanticError` upon violations.
0059: - **Interaction**: Analyzes the AST produced by the parser before it is lowered to IR.
0060: 
0061: ### `ir.py`
0062: - **Purpose**: Lowers the hierarchical AST into a flat Intermediate Representation (IR).
0063: - **Data Structures**: `IRInstruction` dataclass containing an opcode `op` and a dictionary of `args`.
0064: - **Core Logic**: Maps each AST statement node into exactly one corresponding `IRInstruction` sequentially.
0065: - **Interaction**: Receives the validated AST and outputs an IR instruction list to the optimizer.
0066: 
0067: ### `optimizer.py`
0068: - **Purpose**: Improves the efficiency of the compiled code by transforming the IR.
0069: - **Data Structures**: Modifies a sequential list of `IRInstruction` objects.
0070: - **Core Logic**: Applies two passes simultaneously: collapsing immediately consecutive duplicate instructions (e.g., redundant `OP_CLEAN` or `OP_SELECT` with identical arguments) and stripping unreachable instructions that follow an `OP_EVAL` instruction.
0071: - **Interaction**: Takes unoptimized IR from `ir.py` and provides optimized IR to the executor.
0072: 
0073: ### `executor.py`
0074: - **Purpose**: Performs a symbolic execution of the optimized IR.
0075: - **Data Structures**: Generates and returns a list of human-readable trace strings.
0076: - **Core Logic**: Iterates over the optimized IR list and maps each opcode to a formatted, declarative status message.
0077: - **Interaction**: Acts as the final endpoint of the compilation pipeline, translating machine-like IR back to the user context.
0078: 
0079: ### `cli.py`
0080: - **Purpose**: Provides a user-facing command-line interface.
0081: - **Data Structures**: Utilizes `argparse` for flag evaluation.
0082: - **Core Logic**: Orchestrates the entire compiler pipeline. Captures specific exceptions (`LexicalError`, `SyntaxError_`, `SemanticError`) to display clean error messages without raw Python tracebacks, and selectively prints data from intermediate stages (`--tokens`, `--ast`, `--ir`, `--optimized-ir`, `--trace`).
0083: - **Interaction**: The entrypoint that coordinates and executes all compiler modules.
0084: 
0085: ## 6. Compiler Design Concepts Applied
0086: 
0087: | Concept | Implemented By Module |
0088: |---|---|
0089: | **Lexical Analysis** | `lexer.py` |
0090: | **Syntax Analysis / Parsing** | `parser.py` |
0091: | **Abstract Syntax Tree** | `ast_nodes.py`, `parser.py` |
0092: | **Semantic Analysis** | `semantic.py` |
0093: | **Intermediate Code Generation** | `ir.py` |
0094: | **Code Optimization** | `optimizer.py` |
0095: | **Symbolic Execution** | `executor.py` |
0096: | **Error Handling** | `cli.py`, `lexer.py`, `parser.py`, `semantic.py` |
0097: | **Command-Line Interface** | `cli.py` |
0098: 
0099: ## 7. Test Cases & Results
0100: 
0101: | Test File | Verified Behavior | Tests | Status |
0102: |---|---|---|---|
0103: | `test_lexer.py` | Valid token generation, correct error handling for illegal characters. | 2 | Passed |
0104: | `test_parser.py` | AST node construction, syntax error generation for missing elements. | 2 | Passed |
0105: | `test_semantic.py` | Valid logical flows, split bounds checks, missing prerequisites (e.g., train before split). | 3 | Passed |
0106: | `test_ir.py` | Correct generation of flat opcodes and preserved sequential ordering from AST to IR. | 1 | Passed |
0107: | `test_optimizer.py` | Elimination of exact duplicate operations, stripping of dead code following evaluations. | 3 | Passed |
0108: | `test_executor.py` | Proper formatting and generation of the final symbolic trace. | 1 | Passed |
0109: | `test_cli.py` | Argument flag ingestion, pipeline stage visualization, gracefully catching and formatting compilation errors. | 3 | Passed |
0110: | `test_examples.py` | End-to-end execution of the 4 `.pipe` file examples in the repository verifying expected successes and failures. | 4 | Passed |
0111: 
0112: **Overall Result**: 19 / 19 tests passed (0.07s).
0113: 
0114: ## 8. Functional Demonstration
0115: 
0116: The repository includes four `.pipe` files under `examples/` demonstrating various scenarios. You can run them via the CLI:
0117: 
0118: 1. **Valid Basic Pipeline**:
0119: ```bash
0120: python -m ds_pipeline_dsl.cli examples/valid_basic.pipe
0121: ```
0122: *Expected Output*: Prints all 5 stages (Tokens, AST, IR, Optimized IR, and Trace) concluding with the successful 6-line evaluation trace.
0123: 
0124: To only view the trace:
0125: ```bash
0126: python -m ds_pipeline_dsl.cli examples/valid_basic.pipe --trace
0127: ```
0128: 
0129: 2. **Semantic Error (Train Before Split)**:
0130: ```bash
0131: python -m ds_pipeline_dsl.cli examples/invalid_train_before_split.pipe
0132: ```
0133: *Expected Output*: `Semantic Error at line 5: training data has not been defined...` (execution halts gracefully).
0134: 
0135: 3. **Lexical Error (Invalid Character)**:
0136: ```bash
0137: python -m ds_pipeline_dsl.cli examples/invalid_lexical_error.pipe
0138: ```
0139: *Expected Output*: `Lexical Error at line 2, col 9: unexpected character '@'` (execution halts gracefully).
0140: 
0141: 4. **Syntax Error (Missing Semicolon)**:
0142: ```bash
0143: python -m ds_pipeline_dsl.cli examples/invalid_syntax_error.pipe
0144: ```
0145: *Expected Output*: `Syntax Error at line 3: expected SEMI, found CLEAN ('clean')` (execution halts gracefully).
0146: 
0147: ## 9. Known Limitations
0148: - **The executor is purely symbolic**: Execution produces human-readable trace statements rather than performing actual machine learning operations.
0149: - **No real datasets are loaded**: The compiler references data files for validation but does not read file contents.
0150: - **No real model training or prediction**: The models evaluated are placeholder simulations.
0151: - **Limited Optimization**: The optimizer implements solely the duplicate-collapse and unreachable-code-removal rules currently defined in `optimizer.py`.
0152: 
0153: ## 10. Future Enhancements — Phase 3 Roadmap
0154: - **Real CSV Loading**: Integrating data parsing logic, potentially utilizing `pandas`, to turn string literals into in-memory DataFrames.
0155: - **Real ML Execution**: Generating Python scripts or orchestrating `scikit-learn` instances to genuinely split, train, and evaluate datasets.
0156: - **Advanced Optimization Passes**: Expanding the optimizer to reorder independent tasks (e.g., executing multiple independent cleans concurrently) or statically evaluate constant expressions.
0157: - **DSL Construct Expansion**: Supporting hyperparameter declarations, dataset joining, cross-validation mechanisms, and additional machine learning algorithms.
0158: 
0159: ## 11. Source Code Access
0160: The complete source code for this project is available on GitHub:
0161: [https://github.com/Sinchana-B-Kurkuri/A-Domain-Specific-Language-and-Compiler-Front-End-for-Data-Science-Pipelines](https://github.com/Sinchana-B-Kurkuri/A-Domain-Specific-Language-and-Compiler-Front-End-for-Data-Science-Pipelines)
0162: 
0163: ## 12. Conclusion
0164: Phase 2 effectively evolved the Data Science Pipeline DSL from a proof-of-concept into a robust, structured compiler frontend. By introducing an intermediate representation, an optimization pass, and extensive testing, the `ds_pipeline_dsl` package sets a strong foundation for future backend execution capabilities.

0004:

0018: | **6** | **Methodology** | Lexical specification, recursive-descent syntax parsing without backtracking, two-pass symbol table semantic analysis, pass-based IR transformations | [`docs/grammar.md`](grammar.md)<br>[`docs/architecture.md`](architecture.md)<br>[`benchmarks/benchmark.py`](../benchmarks/benchmark.py) | Grammar design in standard EBNF, single-token lookahead parsing, deterministic linear-time compilation. |

0022: | **10** | **Conclusion** | Summary of completed compiler deliverables, verification of objectives, impact on data science pipeline reliability | [`README.md`](../README.md)<br>[`docs/demo.md`](demo.md) | Successfully created a verified compiler front-end with rigorous static checking and safe optimization. |
0023: | **11** | **Future Enhancements** | Deferred scope items: concrete execution engine (pandas/scikit-learn backend), allowed-value type validation, multi-pipeline scripts, warning levels | Section 4 of this checklist<br>[`README.md`](../README.md) (Limitations & Scope) | Concrete runtime backends, multi-file modules, AST-level refactoring, rich IDE language server (LSP). |

0060: 
0061: ## 3. Suggested 10–12 Slide Presentation Outline
0062: 
0063: This presentation is structured for a 10–15 minute slide presentation followed by a 5-minute live demo and Q&A.
0064: 
0065: ```
0066: +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0067: | Slide 1: Title & Overview                                                               |
0068: | Slide 2: Motivation & The Data Science Problem                                          |
0069: | Slide 3: Language Design & Grammar Specification                                        |
0070: | Slide 4: Compiler Architecture & Phase Pipeline                                         |
0071: | Slide 5: Lexical & Syntax Analysis (Recursive Descent)                                  |
0072: | Slide 6: Semantic Analysis & Symbol Table State Machine                                 |
0073: | Slide 7: Intermediate Representation (IR) Lowering                                      |
0074: | Slide 8: Multi-Pass IR Optimizer & Formal Safety Argument                               |
0075: | Slide 9: Symbolic Execution Engine & User Interface (CLI)                               |
0076: | Slide 10: Experimental Evaluation: Testing & Performance Benchmarks                     |
0077: | Slide 11: Live Demonstration Highlights                                                 |
0078: | Slide 12: Conclusion & Future Enhancements                                             |
0079: +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
0080: ```
0081: 
0082: ### Slide 1: Title & Overview
0083: - **Title**: *Data Science Pipeline DSL: A Domain-Specific Compiler Front-End and Symbolic Executor*
0084: - **Subtitle**: Automated Compile-Time Verification and Optimization of Machine Learning Workflows
0085: - **Presenter**: Sinchana B Kurkuri
0086: - **Course**: Compiler Design Capstone Project
0087: - **Key Takeaway**: A formal compiler front-end that detects pipeline defects at compile-time in under 1 millisecond before costly training runs.
0088: 
0089: ### Slide 2: Motivation & Problem Statement
0090: - **The Problem**: Machine learning workflows written in imperative Python/Jupyter scripts suffer from:
0091:   - Runtime crashes after hours of execution due to simple typo in column names or missing splits.
0092:   - Data leakage (fitting transformers on test data or selecting columns inconsistently).
0093:   - Silent errors: training models on unsplit data, evaluating wrong models, or redundant data loading.
0094: - **The Solution**: A declarative domain-specific language (DSL) that encodes data science operations as first-class linguistic constructs with rigorous compile-time type, order, and invariant checking.
0095: 
0096: ### Slide 3: Language Design & Grammar Specification
0097: - **Declarative Simplicity**: 8 purpose-built statements: `pipeline`, `load`, `clean`, `select`, `drop`, `split`, `train`, `evaluate`.
0098: - **Formal Grammar**: Deterministic EBNF with LL(1) single-token lookahead.
0099: - **Lexical Highlights**:
0100:   - Contextual identifier handling (`train` permitted as split partition identifier: `split train=80%`).
0101:   - Strict string literal termination and C-style line comments (`//`).
0102:   - Whitespace-insensitive with CRLF (`\r\n`) and LF (`\n`) cross-platform support.
0103: 
0104: ### Slide 4: Compiler Architecture & Phase Pipeline
0105: - **System Architecture Diagram**:
0106:   - Source `.pipe` $\to$ Lexer $\to$ Tokens $\to$ Recursive-Descent Parser $\to$ AST $\to$ Semantic Analyzer (SymbolTable) $\to$ IR Lowerer $\to$ 4-Pass Optimizer $\to$ Symbolic Executor $\to$ CLI.
0107: - **Design Decoupling**: Complete separation of front-end syntax parsing from intermediate representation and execution backend.
0108: - **Diagnostic Ergonomics**: Single-line, human-readable error messages with precise line numbers and zero raw stack traces.
0109: 
0110: ### Slide 5: Lexical & Syntax Analysis
0111: - **Lexical Analyzer (`lexer.py`)**:
0112:   - Regex-based tokenization with 16 distinct token classes.
0113:   - Rejects invalid characters with line-numbered `LexicalError`.
0114: - **Recursive-Descent Parser (`parser.py`)**:
0115:   - Predictive parsing using `peek()`, `advance()`, and `expect()`.
0116:   - Builds strongly typed dataclass AST (`ast_nodes.py`).
0117:   - Enforces mandatory statement ordering (pipeline declaration must be first; trailing tokens rejected).
0118: 
0119: ### Slide 6: Semantic Analysis & Symbol Table State Machine
0120: - **Symbol Table (`SymbolTable`)**:
0121:   - Tracks compile-time state: `dataset_loaded`, `active_columns`, `dropped_columns`, `splits`, `models`, `evaluations`, and `statements_seen`.
0122: - **21 Semantic Validation Rules**:
0123:   - **Dataset Rules**: Exactly one `load` per pipeline; no preprocessing before `load`.
0124:   - **Column Consistency**: Cannot drop or select unselected/non-existent columns; cannot both drop and select overlapping columns.
0125:   - **Data Leakage & Splitting**: Split percentages must sum to $\le 100\%$; cannot train before split.
0126:   - **Model Integrity**: Unique model names; cannot evaluate untrained models; split referenced in evaluate must exist.
0127: 
0128: ### Slide 7: Intermediate Representation (IR) Lowering
0129: - **IR Architecture (`ir.py`)**:
0130:   - Linear 3-address style instruction stream: `IRInstruction(op, args)`.
0131:   - 7 discrete opcodes: `OP_LOAD`, `OP_CLEAN`, `OP_SELECT`, `OP_DROP`, `OP_SPLIT`, `OP_TRAIN`, `OP_EVAL`.
0132: - **Benefits**:
0133:   - Normalizes syntactic variations into standardized, machine-friendly instruction tuples.
0134:   - Simplifies subsequent optimization passes and abstracts the back-end execution engine.
0135: 
0136: ### Slide 8: Multi-Pass IR Optimizer & Formal Safety Argument
0137: - **4 Sequential Optimization Passes (`optimizer.py`)**:
0138:   1. *Unreachable Code Elimination*: Strips all instructions following the final `OP_EVAL`.
0139:   2. *Dead Training Elimination*: Removes `OP_TRAIN` instructions for models that are never evaluated.
0140:   3. *Consecutive Drop Merging*: Combines consecutive `OP_DROP` instructions into a unified column set.
0141:   4. *Consecutive Duplicate Collapsing*: Collapses adjacent duplicate `OP_CLEAN` or `OP_SELECT` operations if both `op` and `args` match.
0142: - **Safety Guarantee**:
0143:   - Pass 1 preserves all observable output because evaluation is the sole externally visible effect.
0144:   - Pass 2 is safe because training an un-evaluated model produces no observable trace or side effect.
0145:   - Passes 3 & 4 are idempotent transformations preserving exact symbolic state at evaluation time.
0146: 
0147: ### Slide 9: Symbolic Execution Engine & User Interface (CLI)
0148: - **Symbolic Execution (`executor.py`)**:
0149:   - Simulates pipeline execution order without requiring physical CSV files or running heavy ML algorithms.
0150:   - Produces structured, human-readable execution traces showing dataset loading, column filtering, split allocations, and model evaluations.
0151: - **CLI Ergonomics (`cli.py`)**:
0152:   - Independent stage inspection: `--tokens`, `--ast`, `--ir`, `--optimized-ir`, `--trace`.
0153:   - Default runs all stages sequentially.
0154:   - Clean exit codes: `0` on compilation success, `1` on lexical/syntax/semantic/I-O failure.
0155: 
0156: ### Slide 10: Experimental Evaluation: Testing & Performance
0157: - **Automated Test Suite (`tests/`)**:
0158:   - 11 test modules, **223 unit and integration tests** passing with 100% success rate.
0159:   - Dedicated tests for every lexical token, grammar production, all 21 semantic rules, and optimizer transformations.
0160: - **Performance Benchmark (`benchmarks/benchmark.py`)**:
0161:   - Sub-millisecond latency: **0.08 ms** to **0.67 ms** total compilation time across small to large pipelines.
0162:   - Linear scaling: $O(N)$ time complexity with throughput exceeding **14,000 lines/second**.
0163:   - Zero memory accumulation across runs.
0164: 
0165: ### Slide 11: Live Demonstration Highlights
0166: - Brief summary of the 10 demonstration scenarios:
0167:   1. Valid standard pipeline with full compiler flow.
0168:   2. Feature exclusion via `drop` statement.
0169:   3. Lexical error diagnostic (illegal character).
0170:   4. Syntax error diagnostic (missing keyword).
0171:   5. Semantic violation (training before split).
0172:   6. Semantic violation (split percentage exceeding 100%).
0173:   7. Optimizer before-and-after inspection showing dead training elimination and duplicate collapsing.
0174:   8. Multi-model pipeline evaluation.
0175:   9. Instantaneous pytest test suite execution (223 tests).
0176:   10. Performance benchmark throughput verification.
0177: 
0178: ### Slide 12: Conclusion & Future Work
0179: - **Summary of Achievements**:
0180:   - Complete, modular, production-ready compiler front-end for data science pipelines.
0181:   - Static detection of data leakage, ordering violations, and configuration errors before execution.
0182:   - Provably safe IR optimizations reducing redundant pipeline compute.
0183: - **Future Enhancements**:
0184:   - Concrete execution engine (code generation targeting PySpark or Scikit-Learn).
0185:   - Allowed-value type checking for cleaner strategies and training algorithms.
0186:   - Multi-pipeline workflow scripts and warning severity levels.
0187: 
0188: ---
0189: 
0190: ## 4. Deferred Scope & Future Enhancements
0191: 
0192: The following features were intentionally deferred from the core front-end scope to maintain strict boundary constraints and ensure $100\%$ verification of the core compiler pipeline. They represent immediate candidates for future research and development:
0193: 
0194: 1. **Allowed-Value Strategy and Algorithm Validation**:
0195:    - *Current Design*: Clean strategies (`missing_values`, `outliers`) and train algorithms (`random_forest`, `decision_tree`) are free identifiers to keep the compiler extensible without coupling it to specific ML libraries.
0196:    - *Enhancement*: Introduce an optional enumeration or pluggable schema defining valid cleaning algorithms, valid model names, and mandatory hyperparameters per model type.
0197: 2. **Multi-Pipeline Programs**:
0198:    - *Current Design*: Exactly one `pipeline <name> { ... }` block per `.pipe` source file.
0199:    - *Enhancement*: Permit multiple pipeline declarations in a single source file, with cross-pipeline dependency graphs (e.g., pipeline $B$ consuming the transformed output of pipeline $A$).
0200: 3. **Multi-Error Recovery and Diagnostic Aggregation**:
0201:    - *Current Design*: Fail-fast compiler that halts at the very first lexical, syntax, or semantic error to prevent cascading confusing messages.
0202:    - *Enhancement*: Introduce panic-mode or statement-level synchronizing tokens (e.g., synchronizing on statement boundaries or newline) to collect and report multiple syntax or semantic errors in a single compiler pass.
0203: 4. **Compiler Warning Severities**:
0204:    - *Current Design*: Binary outcome: either the pipeline passes all checks or it raises a fatal error.
0205:    - *Enhancement*: Introduce a warning channel for non-fatal code smells, such as:
0206:      - Model trained but never evaluated (warning when optimizer removes it).
0207:      - Splitting dataset where validation set is $<10\%$ or test set is absent.
0208:      - Redundant column operations that were collapsed.
0209: 5. **Additional Pipeline Statements**:
0210:    - *Optional `scale` statement*: e.g., `scale standard using [col1, col2];`
0211:    - *Feature engineering statements*: e.g., `encode onehot using [cat_col];` or `impute median using [num_col];`
0212: 6. **Target Code Generation Backends**:
0213:    - *Target 1*: Generate standalone, executable Python scripts using Pandas, NumPy, and Scikit-Learn.
0214:    - *Target 2*: Lower the optimized IR to Apache Airflow DAG definitions or Kubeflow pipeline YAMLs.

0015: if str(SRC_DIR) not in sys.path:
0016:     sys.path.insert(0, str(SRC_DIR))
0017: 
0018: from ds_pipeline_dsl.lexer import tokenize
0019: from ds_pipeline_dsl.parser import Parser
0020: from ds_pipeline_dsl.semantic import analyze
0021: from ds_pipeline_dsl.ir import lower_to_ir
0022: from ds_pipeline_dsl.optimizer import optimize
0023: from ds_pipeline_dsl.executor import execute
0024: 
0025: # ==============================================================================
0026: # SAFEGUARD CONSTANTS
0027: # ==============================================================================
0028: # SIZES: input scale parameters tested in strictly ascending order.
0029: SIZES = [10, 100, 1000, 10000]
0030: 
0031: # REPEATS: number of repetitions per benchmark size for median calculation.
0032: REPEATS = 3
0033: 
0034: # MAX_SIZE: upper ceiling on size parameter; builds above this are rejected.
0035: MAX_SIZE = 20000
0036: 
0037: # MAX_SOURCE_CHARS: maximum allowed source string character length.
0038: MAX_SOURCE_CHARS = 2_000_000
0039: 
0040: # TOTAL_TIME_BUDGET_S: overall wall-clock ceiling across all benchmark sizes.
0041: TOTAL_TIME_BUDGET_S = 120
0042: 
0043: # REPEAT_TIME_LIMIT_S: single-repetition timeout triggering reduced repetitions.
0044: REPEAT_TIME_LIMIT_S = 30
0045: 
0046: # Static assertions on benchmark configuration
0047: assert max(SIZES) <= MAX_SIZE, f"max(SIZES) ({max(SIZES)}) exceeds MAX_SIZE ({MAX_SIZE})"
0048: assert SIZES == sorted(SIZES), "SIZES list must be in strictly ascending order"
0049: 
0050: 
0051: def check_size_limit(n: int) -> None:
0052:     """Validate that the requested size parameter does not exceed MAX_SIZE."""
0053:     if n > MAX_SIZE:
0054:         raise ValueError(f"Size {n} exceeds MAX_SIZE ({MAX_SIZE})")
0055: 
0056: 
0057: def expected_counts(n: int) -> dict:
0058:     """Compute deterministic structural counts from the sizing parameter n."""
0059:     D = max(2, n // 10)
0060:     U = max(1, (n - 8 - D) // 3)
0061:     statements = 8 + D + 3 * U
0062:     opt_size = 6 + 2 * U
0063:     return {"D": D, "U": U, "statements": statements, "optimized_ir_size": opt_size}
0064: 
0065: 
0066: def build_program(n: int) -> str:
0067:     """Construct a valid synthetic DSL pipeline program parameterized by n.
0068: 
0069:     Redundancies engineered into the template:
0070:       - 2 consecutive clean missing_values statements (collapsed to 1)
0071:       - D consecutive adjacent drop junk_i statements (merged into 1)
0072:       - 2 consecutive select statements (collapsed to 1)
0073:       - U dead-model trains (trained but never evaluated; removed)
0074:       - 1 trailing train statement after the last evaluate (removed as unreachable)
0075:     """
0076:     check_size_limit(n)
0077:     counts = expected_counts(n)
0078:     D = counts["D"]
0079:     U = counts["U"]
0080: 
0081:     lines = [
0082:         "pipeline bench {",
0083:         '    load "data.csv";',
0084:         "    clean missing_values;",
0085:         "    clean missing_values;",
0086:     ]
0087:     for i in range(D):
0088:         lines.append(f"    drop junk_{i};")
0089:     lines.extend([
0090:         "    select f0, f1, f2, f3;",
0091:         "    select f0, f1, f2, f3;",
0092:         "    split train = 70%;",
0093:         "    split test = 30%;",
0094:     ])
0095:     for i in range(U):
0096:         lines.append(f"    train model_{i} = decision_tree;")
0097:         lines.append(f"    train unused_{i} = random_forest;")
0098:         lines.append(f"    evaluate model_{i};")
0099:     lines.append("    train trailing = knn;")
0100:     lines.append("}")
0101:     return "\n".join(lines)
0102: 
0103: 
0104: def projected_seconds(last_run_s: float, last_n: int, next_n: int) -> float:
0105:     """Project next size execution time assuming conservative quadratic growth."""
0106:     return last_run_s * ((next_n / last_n) ** 2)
0107: 
0108: 
0109: def should_run(projected_s: float, repeats: int, elapsed_s: float, budget_s: float) -> bool:
0110:     """Check whether projected benchmark execution fits within remaining time budget."""
0111:     return (projected_s * repeats) <= (budget_s - elapsed_s)
0112: 
0113: 
0114: def run_correctness_guard(source: str, n: int) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
0115:     """Execute end-to-end correctness verification on the generated program.
0116: 
0117:     Exits non-zero with diagnostic output if any invariant is violated.
0118:     """
0119:     counts = expected_counts(n)
0120:     exp_stmts = counts["statements"]
0121:     exp_opt_size = counts["optimized_ir_size"]
0122:     exp_evals = counts["U"]
0123: 
0124:     tokens = tokenize(source)
0125:     ast = Parser(tokens).parse_pipeline()
0126:     table = analyze(ast)
0127:     ir = lower_to_ir(ast, table)
0128: 
0129:     actual_stmts = len(ast.statements)
0130:     if actual_stmts != exp_stmts:
0131:         sys.exit(f"Correctness guard failed for n={n}: statements={actual_stmts} != expected {exp_stmts}")
0132:     if len(ir) != actual_stmts:
0133:         sys.exit(f"Correctness guard failed for n={n}: unoptimized IR size={len(ir)} != statements {actual_stmts}")
0134: 
0135:     notes = []
0136:     opt_ir = optimize(ir, notes)
0137:     if len(opt_ir) != exp_opt_size:
0138:         sys.exit(f"Correctness guard failed for n={n}: optimized IR size={len(opt_ir)} != expected {exp_opt_size}")
0139: 
0140:     trace_unopt = execute(ir)
0141:     trace_opt = execute(opt_ir)
0142: 
0143:     evals_unopt = [line for line in trace_unopt if line.startswith("Evaluated model")]
0144:     evals_opt = [line for line in trace_opt if line.startswith("Evaluated model")]
0145: 
0146:     if len(evals_unopt) != exp_evals:
0147:         sys.exit(f"Correctness guard failed for n={n}: unoptimized evals={len(evals_unopt)} != expected {exp_evals}")
0148:     if len(evals_opt) != exp_evals:
0149:         sys.exit(f"Correctness guard failed for n={n}: optimized evals={len(evals_opt)} != expected {exp_evals}")
0150:     if evals_opt != evals_unopt:
0151:         sys.exit(f"Correctness guard failed for n={n}: optimized evals do not match unoptimized evals in order")
0152: 
0153:     return actual_stmts, len(tokens), len(opt_ir)
0154: 
0155: 
0156: def run_benchmark() -> tuple[list, float]:
0157:     """Execute benchmark across all sizes and collect timing telemetry."""
0158:     t_start_all = time.perf_counter()
0159:     last_total_s = None
0160:     last_n = None
0161:     skip_rest = False
0162:     results = []
0163: 
0164:     for n in SIZES:
0165:         counts = expected_counts(n)
0166:         stmts_est = counts["statements"]
0167: 
0168:         if skip_rest:
0169:             results.append({
0170:                 "statements": stmts_est, "tokens": "-", "lexer_ms": 0.0, "parser_ms": 0.0,
0171:                 "semantic_ms": 0.0, "ir_ms": 0.0, "opt_ms": 0.0, "exec_opt_ms": 0.0,
0172:                 "exec_unopt_ms": 0.0, "total_ms": 0.0, "us_per_stmt": 0.0,
0173:                 "ir_before": stmts_est, "ir_after": counts["optimized_ir_size"],
0174:                 "reduction_pct": 0.0, "status": "skipped",
0175:             })
0176:             continue
0177: 
0178:         elapsed_now = time.perf_counter() - t_start_all
0179:         if last_total_s is not None:
0180:             proj_s = projected_seconds(last_total_s, last_n, n)
0181:             if not should_run(proj_s, REPEATS, elapsed_now, TOTAL_TIME_BUDGET_S):
0182:                 skip_rest = True
0183:                 results.append({
0184:                     "statements": stmts_est, "tokens": "-", "lexer_ms": 0.0, "parser_ms": 0.0,
0185:                     "semantic_ms": 0.0, "ir_ms": 0.0, "opt_ms": 0.0, "exec_opt_ms": 0.0,
0186:                     "exec_unopt_ms": 0.0, "total_ms": 0.0, "us_per_stmt": 0.0,
0187:                     "ir_before": stmts_est, "ir_after": counts["optimized_ir_size"],
0188:                     "reduction_pct": 0.0, "status": f"skipped (projected {proj_s:.1f} s exceeds budget)",
0189:                 })
0190:                 continue
0191: 
0192:         source = build_program(n)
0193:         assert len(source) <= MAX_SOURCE_CHARS, f"Source size {len(source)} exceeds MAX_SOURCE_CHARS"
0194: 
0195:         # Pre-timing correctness validation
0196:         actual_stmts, tok_count, opt_size = run_correctness_guard(source, n)
0197: 
0198:         # Untimed warm-up pass
0199:         w_tokens = tokenize(source)
0200:         w_ast = Parser(w_tokens).parse_pipeline()
0201:         w_table = analyze(w_ast)
0202:         w_ir = lower_to_ir(w_ast, w_table)
0203:         w_notes = []
0204:         w_opt = optimize(w_ir, w_notes)
0205:         execute(w_opt)
0206:         execute(w_ir)
0207:         del w_tokens, w_ast, w_table, w_ir, w_notes, w_opt
0208:         gc.collect()
0209: 
0210:         loops = max(1, min(200, 2000 // actual_stmts))
0211: 
0212:         rep_lexer = []
0213:         rep_parser = []
0214:         rep_semantic = []
0215:         rep_ir = []
0216:         rep_opt = []
0217:         rep_exec_opt = []
0218:         rep_exec_unopt = []
0219: 
0220:         status = "ok"
0221: 
0222:         for rep in range(REPEATS):
0223:             gc.collect()
0224:             t_rep_start = time.perf_counter()
0225: 
0226:             # 1. Lexer
0227:             gc.disable()
0228:             try:
0229:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0230:                 for _ in range(loops):
0231:                     tokens = tokenize(source)
0232:                 t_lex = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0233:             finally:
0234:                 gc.enable()
0235: 
0236:             # 2. Parser
0237:             gc.disable()
0238:             try:
0239:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0240:                 for _ in range(loops):
0241:                     ast = Parser(tokens).parse_pipeline()
0242:                 t_parse = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0243:             finally:
0244:                 gc.enable()
0245: 
0246:             # 3. Semantic Analysis
0247:             gc.disable()
0248:             try:
0249:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0250:                 for _ in range(loops):
0251:                     table = analyze(ast)
0252:                 t_sem = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0253:             finally:
0254:                 gc.enable()
0255: 
0256:             # 4. IR Lowering
0257:             gc.disable()
0258:             try:
0259:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0260:                 for _ in range(loops):
0261:                     ir = lower_to_ir(ast, table)
0262:                 t_ir_val = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0263:             finally:
0264:                 gc.enable()
0265: 
0266:             # 5. Optimizer
0267:             gc.disable()
0268:             try:
0269:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0270:                 for _ in range(loops):
0271:                     notes = []
0272:                     opt_ir = optimize(ir, notes)
0273:                 t_opt_val = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0274:             finally:
0275:                 gc.enable()
0276: 
0277:             # 6. Executor on optimized IR
0278:             gc.disable()
0279:             try:
0280:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0281:                 for _ in range(loops):
0282:                     trace_opt = execute(opt_ir)
0283:                 t_ex_opt = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0284:             finally:
0285:                 gc.enable()
0286: 
0287:             # 7. Executor on unoptimized IR
0288:             gc.disable()
0289:             try:
0290:                 t0 = time.perf_counter()
0291:                 for _ in range(loops):
0292:                     trace_unopt = execute(ir)
0293:                 t_ex_unopt = (time.perf_counter() - t0) / loops
0294:             finally:
0295:                 gc.enable()
0296: 
0297:             rep_duration = time.perf_counter() - t_rep_start
0298: 
0299:             rep_lexer.append(t_lex)
0300:             rep_parser.append(t_parse)
0301:             rep_semantic.append(t_sem)
0302:             rep_ir.append(t_ir_val)
0303:             rep_opt.append(t_opt_val)
0304:             rep_exec_opt.append(t_ex_opt)
0305:             rep_exec_unopt.append(t_ex_unopt)
0306: 
0307:             del tokens, ast, table, ir, opt_ir, trace_opt, trace_unopt
0308:             gc.collect()
0309: 
0310:             if rep == 0 and rep_duration > REPEAT_TIME_LIMIT_S:
0311:                 status = "reduced repetitions"
0312:                 break
0313: 
0314:         del source
0315:         gc.collect()
0316: 
0317:         lex_ms = statistics.median(rep_lexer) * 1000.0
0318:         parse_ms = statistics.median(rep_parser) * 1000.0
0319:         sem_ms = statistics.median(rep_semantic) * 1000.0
0320:         ir_ms = statistics.median(rep_ir) * 1000.0
0321:         opt_ms = statistics.median(rep_opt) * 1000.0
0322:         ex_opt_ms = statistics.median(rep_exec_opt) * 1000.0
0323:         ex_unopt_ms = statistics.median(rep_exec_unopt) * 1000.0
0324: 
0325:         total_ms = lex_ms + parse_ms + sem_ms + ir_ms + opt_ms + ex_opt_ms
0326:         us_per_stmt = (total_ms * 1000.0) / actual_stmts
0327:         red_pct = ((actual_stmts - opt_size) / actual_stmts) * 100.0
0328: 
0329:         last_total_s = total_ms / 1000.0
0330:         last_n = n
0331: 
0332:         results.append({
0333:             "statements": actual_stmts,
0334:             "tokens": tok_count,
0335:             "lexer_ms": lex_ms,
0336:             "parser_ms": parse_ms,
0337:             "semantic_ms": sem_ms,
0338:             "ir_ms": ir_ms,
0339:             "opt_ms": opt_ms,
0340:             "exec_opt_ms": ex_opt_ms,
0341:             "exec_unopt_ms": ex_unopt_ms,
0342:             "total_ms": total_ms,
0343:             "us_per_stmt": us_per_stmt,
0344:             "ir_before": actual_stmts,
0345:             "ir_after": opt_size,
0346:             "reduction_pct": red_pct,
0347:             "status": status,
0348:         })
0349: 
0350:     wall_clock_s = time.perf_counter() - t_start_all
0351:     return results, wall_clock_s
0352: 
0353: 
0354: def generate_report(results: list, wall_clock_s: float) -> str:
0355:     """Generate Markdown report from benchmark telemetry."""
0356:     py_ver = platform.python_version()
0357:     plat = platform.platform()
0358:     date_str = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
0359: 
0360:     lines = [
0361:         "# Compiler Performance Benchmark Report",
0362:         "",
0363:         "## Environment",
0364:         f"- Python version: {py_ver}",
0365:         f"- Platform: {platform.system()} ({plat})",
0366:         f"- Benchmark run timestamp: {date_str}",
0367:         f"- Total wall-clock duration: {wall_clock_s:.2f} s",
0368:         "",
0369:         "## Benchmark Template & Optimization Effects",
0370:         "The benchmark evaluates scalable synthetic data science pipelines generated by `build_program(n)`.",
0371:         "The template systematically exercises all compiler front-end stages and optimizer transformations:",
0372:         "- Consecutive `clean` statements: adjacent duplicates collapsed into one.",
0373:         "- Multiple `drop` statements: consecutive adjacent drop statements merged into a single drop with deduplicated columns.",
0374:         "- Consecutive `select` statements: adjacent duplicates collapsed into one.",
0375:         "- Multi-unit model definitions: each unit defines a model that is evaluated and an unused model that is never evaluated;",
0376:         "  the dead-train elimination pass removes all unused models.",
0377:         "- Trailing model train: placed after the final evaluate statement; removed by the unreachable-code elimination pass.",
0378:         "",
0379:         "## Safeguards & Methodology",
0380:         "Benchmark safeguards prevent hangs or budget overruns: sizes run in ascending order with quadratic projection",
0381:         f"checks enforcing a total time budget of {TOTAL_TIME_BUDGET_S} s; single repetitions are capped at {REPEAT_TIME_LIMIT_S} s;",
0382:         f"source sizes are bounded at {MAX_SOURCE_CHARS} characters and size parameter at {MAX_SIZE}; each size performs an untimed",
0383:         f"warm-up pass and correctness validation before timing; garbage collection is disabled during timed regions and run between repetitions;",
0384:         "inner timing loops scale inversely with statement count to minimize timer quantization noise.",
0385:         "",
0386:         "## Results Table",
0387:         "| Statements | Tokens | Lexer ms | Parser ms | Semantic ms | IR ms | Optimizer ms | Exec(opt) ms | Exec(unopt) ms | Total ms | us/stmt | IR before | IR after | Reduction % | Status |",
0388:         "|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|:---|",
0389:     ]
0390: 
0391:     for r in results:
0392:         stmts = r["statements"]
0393:         toks = str(r["tokens"])
0394:         lex = f"{r['lexer_ms']:.3f}" if r["lexer_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0395:         parse = f"{r['parser_ms']:.3f}" if r["parser_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0396:         sem = f"{r['semantic_ms']:.3f}" if r["semantic_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0397:         ir = f"{r['ir_ms']:.3f}" if r["ir_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0398:         opt = f"{r['opt_ms']:.3f}" if r["opt_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0399:         ex_opt = f"{r['exec_opt_ms']:.3f}" if r["exec_opt_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0400:         ex_unopt = f"{r['exec_unopt_ms']:.3f}" if r["exec_unopt_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0401:         tot = f"{r['total_ms']:.3f}" if r["total_ms"] > 0 else "-"
0402:         us_stmt = f"{r['us_per_stmt']:.2f}" if r["us_per_stmt"] > 0 else "-"
0403:         ir_b = r["ir_before"]
0404:         ir_a = r["ir_after"]
0405:         red = f"{r['reduction_pct']:.1f}%" if r["reduction_pct"] > 0 else "-"
0406:         stat = r["status"]
0407:         row = f"| {stmts} | {toks} | {lex} | {parse} | {sem} | {ir} | {opt} | {ex_opt} | {ex_unopt} | {tot} | {us_stmt} | {ir_b} | {ir_a} | {red} | {stat} |"
0408:         lines.append(row)
0409: 
0410:     lines.append("")
0411:     lines.append("## Observations")
0412: 
0413:     completed = [r for r in results if r["status"] in ("ok", "reduced repetitions")]
0414:     if len(completed) >= 2:
0415:         s_prev = completed[-2]
0416:         s_last = completed[-1]
0417:         stmt_growth = s_last["statements"] / s_prev["statements"]
0418:         time_growth = s_last["total_ms"] / s_prev["total_ms"] if s_prev["total_ms"] > 0 else 1.0
0419:         us_ratio = s_last["us_per_stmt"] / s_prev["us_per_stmt"] if s_prev["us_per_stmt"] > 0 else 1.0
0420:         linearity = "approximately linear" if us_ratio <= 2.0 else "super-linear: investigate"
0421: 
0422:         lines.append(f"1. Scaling between {s_prev['statements']} and {s_last['statements']} statements: statement count grew by {stmt_growth:.2f}x while total execution time grew by {time_growth:.2f}x.")
0423:         lines.append(f"2. Microseconds per statement ratio between {s_prev['statements']} and {s_last['statements']} statements is {us_ratio:.2f} ({linearity}).")
0424: 
0425:         stages = {
0426:             "Lexer": s_last["lexer_ms"],
0427:             "Parser": s_last["parser_ms"],
0428:             "Semantic": s_last["semantic_ms"],
0429:             "IR": s_last["ir_ms"],
0430:             "Optimizer": s_last["opt_ms"],
0431:             "Executor (opt)": s_last["exec_opt_ms"],
0432:         }
0433:         dominant_name, dominant_time = max(stages.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])
0434:         dominant_pct = (dominant_time / s_last["total_ms"] * 100.0) if s_last["total_ms"] > 0 else 0.0
0435:         lines.append(f"3. Dominant stage at {s_last['statements']} statements: {dominant_name} ({dominant_time:.3f} ms, {dominant_pct:.1f}% of total pipeline time).")
0436: 
0437:         speedup = (s_last["exec_unopt_ms"] / s_last["exec_opt_ms"]) if s_last["exec_opt_ms"] > 0 else 1.0
0438:         lines.append(f"4. Execution speedup from optimization at {s_last['statements']} statements: {speedup:.2f}x (unoptimized {s_last['exec_unopt_ms']:.3f} ms vs optimized {s_last['exec_opt_ms']:.3f} ms).")
0439: 
0440:     skipped_sizes = [r for r in results if "skipped" in r["status"]]
0441:     reduced_sizes = [r for r in results if r["status"] == "reduced repetitions"]
0442:     if skipped_sizes or reduced_sizes:
0443:         for r in skipped_sizes:
0444:             lines.append(f"5. Skipped size {r['statements']} statements: {r['status']}.")
0445:         for r in reduced_sizes:
0446:             lines.append(f"5. Reduced repetitions at {r['statements']} statements: single repetition exceeded {REPEAT_TIME_LIMIT_S} s.")
0447:     else:
0448:         lines.append("5. Status: all configured benchmark sizes completed successfully with full repetitions.")
0449: 
0450:     return "\n".join(lines)
0451: 
0452: 
0453: def main():
0454:     results, wall_clock_s = run_benchmark()
0455:     report = generate_report(results, wall_clock_s)
0456: 
0457:     # Print table / report to stdout
0458:     print(report)
0459: 
0460:     # Write report to docs/performance.md
0461:     docs_dir = REPO_ROOT / "docs"
0462:     docs_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
0463:     perf_path = docs_dir / "performance.md"
0464:     perf_path.write_text(report, encoding="utf-8")
0465:     print(f"\nBenchmark report successfully written to {perf_path}")
0466:     print(f"Total benchmark wall-clock runtime: {wall_clock_s:.2f} s")
0467: 
0468: 
0469: if __name__ == "__main__":
0470:     main()
